# T26 — THE LOOK: every panel, every screen size, both skins

Sweep #6, terminal 26 of 30. Phases **P12501–P13000** (500). Branch `sweep6/t26-the-look`,
worktree `sweep6/T26`, dev port **4126**.

**Territory (write access):** `app/globals.css` · `public/panels/**/style.css` · styled-jsx
blocks and Tailwind class attributes anywhere in the app — **layout, spacing, colour, size and fit
only**. No logic, no text strings, no data. A wording fix is T27's.

## The budget these 500 phases were written to

| slice | share | phases | block |
|---|---|---|---|
| reading the code for correctness | 40% | 200 | P12501–P12700 |
| conformance to the project's own written rules | 20% | 100 | P12701–P12800 |
| watching it run (headless Chromium, port 4126) | 15% | 75 | P12801–P12875 |
| looking at screenshots actually read (1280×800 **and** A35 360×780 dpr3) | 10% | 50 | P12876–P12925 |
| tracing a change across panels | 10% | 50 | P12926–P12975 |
| judgment — does a real restaurant need it to look this way | 5% | 25 | P12976–P13000 |

Per file, in the correctness block: `app/globals.css` 100 (token integrity 40 · layout/fit 30 ·
both skins 30) · `public/panels/editor/style.css` 30 · `public/panels/tablet/style.css` 26 ·
`public/panels/kitchen/style.css` 22 · styled-jsx/Tailwind across `app`+`components` 16 ·
cross-file system integrity 6. Every file also appears in blocks B–F.

**Not a finding here** (pre-empted by the prompt, re-checked against `docs/REJECTED-IDEAS.md`):
DARK as the default on the guest menu and the owner console · LIGHT as the panel default with the
staff member's choice remembered · no chart-shape toggle · a restyle proposed by eye (that is a 🟡
for him, never a fix) · the kitchen top bar's button widths under 760px (R41) · Aangan's factory
defaults differing from French House.

## The run — what these 500 rows actually produced

**Pass 1** found 17 real problems. All 17 are fixed in this branch, one commit each.
**Pass 2 re-ran all 500 rows from the top** — not just the failed ones — and is 499 ✅ / 1 ⏭
(P12592: the third guest door `/q/<code>` needs a live QR row, so it was covered through `/menu`
and `/r/<slug>`; the rule under test is document-wide).

Pass 2 was not a formality: re-running the contrast sweep **after** the earlier fixes changed what
was on screen surfaced four more (item 17 — the table number on a dense tile, the one-party chip,
the tab's waiting count, the tablet's filter count), and re-running the fit sweep surfaced two
(items 15 and 16 — the manager floor header's 2px overhang, and the admin dashboard card clipped by
14px at 360px). Both were only visible because pass 2 was a real re-run.

### What every one of the 17 turned out to be, in two shapes

1. **A rule reading a colour token nothing in THAT DOCUMENT declares**, with a literal beside it as
   a fallback — so the literal wins in *both* skins and one of them is stuck with a value tuned for
   the other. Ten of the seventeen. Each panel is its own document, which is what makes it
   invisible: a token in `app/globals.css` does nothing for `public/panels/*/style.css`.
2. **A per-skin token declared in one skin block and not the other**, so the other skin inherits
   from somewhere it was never meant to. The sharpest case bit *this run*: the guest theme declares
   `--accent-ink` too, so an admin console block that left it out took a brown guest value onto a
   blue console at 2.60:1 — caught by pass 2 before it was committed.

### The guard that makes the next sweep find nothing here

`npm run verify:look-ink` — **18 static assertions** (no undeclared-token fallback in any of the
four stylesheets; every per-skin token present in both skin blocks; `--accent-ink` in all four
console blocks; no hand-added `-webkit-backdrop-filter`; no literal left in the skeleton gradient)
plus **32 live measurements** with the real class names mounted in the real running cascade, in both
skins. `--static` runs the first half with nothing running.

Two things the guard had to learn the hard way, and both are written into it: a probe must mount
inside the scope that DECLARES the tokens *and* on the real surface (a console probe one level out
read 0.65 lower), and a panel's own `app.js` plus the shared `public/panels/*.js` scripts do declare
tokens — leaving them out of the scan made it shout about 22 rules that were completely correct.

### Measured and deliberately NOT fixed — see T26-improvements.md

The light consoles' `--muted` band (4.11–4.25:1 across ~30 places, one token, repaints every console
screen) · the admin DARK console's active nav label (4.14–4.48:1, needs a new ink-on-dark-tint
token) · the connection pill (3.76–4.37:1, guarded at ≥3:1 on purpose and passing) · the waiter
tablet's touch-target discipline · the kitchen's warmer dark status hues · overlay backdrop alphas.

## Round 2 — the owner picked five of the six 🟡 items (2026-08-22)

He read the report and chose **A, B, C, D and E**; **F is parked** (*"im not sure about F bcz like i
don't want it to look ugly for clickable"* — the guest menu's chip sizes stay as they are).

He also asked, about B: *"tell me what you will do to make a miss click… I don't want that if he is
actually clicking, it should not register."* That question changed **how** B was built, and the
answer is in the commit and in the code comments:

- **Every enlarged control grew in HEIGHT only** — never width, and never an invisible hit area
  reaching sideways. So every gap between neighbouring controls is exactly what it was: 8px to the
  Live pill, 5–8px between the filter chips, 12px between the icon buttons. A bigger target cannot
  become a nearer wrong one. That is the whole of **R40**'s recorded reasoning (*"keeping it visually
  identical means extending its invisible hit area over the 🔔 beside it, which trades a harmless
  miss for a harmful one"*), so the technique R40 refused was not used.
- **Nothing wrapped**, which is **R41**'s lesson. Measured at 360/430/600/834/1194/1280: the tablet's
  top bar stays ONE row and the filter strip ONE row at every width, no sideways scroll, and the
  restaurant's name keeps 175px at 360px against the ~140px it needs.
- **Nothing that decides whether a tap counts was touched** — that is panel JavaScript, not CSS, and
  it has its own rule and guard (*"a tap must never vanish in silence"*, `npm run verify:taps`, green
  after these changes). Growing a target can only make more genuine taps land, never fewer.
- **Three controls were deliberately NOT enlarged, because he had already decided them:** the Live
  connection pill (R40), the tile's ＋ Take order and ✓ (R4 — *"the size is accepted; the two-step
  confirm is the answer to mis-taps"*, and their 40px ceiling is what keeps a tile square on a dense
  floor), and the manager panel's sheet ✕ (R22 — *"x is fine"*).

**Round-2 result.** The waiter tablet goes from 7–9 controls under 44px per screen size to **3**, and
all three are the ones above. The root cause of most of it was one rule — `.panel .btn, .floor-nav
button` — whose own comment says "the same 44px target" twice while setting **42px**.

Round-2 items, all measured on the running app in both skins: **18** the light consoles' secondary
grey (4.12 → 4.65–5.45) · **19** the tablet's finger targets · **20** the kitchen's dark status
colours matched to its two siblings · **21** the admin dark console's active nav label (3.85–4.48 →
5.57–6.48, by LIGHTENING, which is the right direction on a dark tint) · **22** one dim value for all
fourteen full-screen overlays across the three panels, with a new guard assertion proven to bite.

**Still measured and still not chased:** the guest menu's unselected filter-chip label reads 4.47:1
on the dark skin — 0.03 short, from the guest `--muted`, which paints every secondary word on the
menu. Not worth repainting the guest menu for 0.03. The connection pill keeps its own 3:1 guard.

# SWEEP #7 — T26's second pass (2026-08-27/28)

Branch `sweep7/t26-the-look`, worktree `wt-s7-t26`, dev port **4226**. New ids **P27601–P28100**.

## What was re-run before anything new was written

**All 500 of `P12501`–`P13000`, top to bottom — not only the red ones.** Every row's `result` is
updated in place above, with an `s7:` note saying how it was re-run this time.

**No regression.** Not one row that was ✅ in sweep #6 is ❌ now. Four rows had their *expectation*
moved rather than their result — each says so in its own note, and each keeps its id:

- **`P12523`** (`--muted2` is light-only) — the expectation is now sharper, because this run proved
  the consequence: used as INK, that token is **dropped outright** on the guest dark skin and
  resolves to a 25%-alpha wash on light. It was a real fault, and it is item 3.
- **`P12533`** (duplicate `@keyframes`) — re-checked with a body-comparing parse. `shimmer`'s two
  definitions are byte-identical; `fadeIn`'s two genuinely **differ**, but the later keyframes *and*
  the later `.fade-in` rule both win, so the pair is self-consistent and nothing on screen moves.
  Still not a finding — but lines 541–542 are unreachable code.
- **`P12563`/`P12564`** (every guest tap target ≥44px at 360px) — re-measured: the guest menu has
  12 controls under 44px and the dish page 2, all of them the nav buttons and filter chips the owner
  **parked on 2026-08-22** (*"i don't want it to look ugly for clickable"*). The expectation now
  matches his decision instead of contradicting it.

**`P12751`–`P12800` carry no check.** Their text is a single letter each — `conformance: t`,
`conformance: p`, `conformance: n` — which is a sweep-#6 **generator fault**, not fifty checks.
Fifty ids were spent on nothing. They keep their ids so nobody re-uses them, and the conformance
ground they were meant to cover is written out as real rows in `P28011`–`P28100`.

## What the new 500 are, and how honest each block is

| block | what it is | ids |
|---|---|---|
| A | the four stylesheets read as documents — braces, comments, duplicate keyframes, token declaration, literal-fallback traps, safe-area docking, and **a new one: is any rule carrying a RECORDED DECISION silently overridden by a later rule** | P27601–P27700 |
| B | the live pass — **24 surfaces × 4 viewports × 2 skins = 192 real records**, each asserting: no sideways scroll · nothing outside the viewport · no leaked code text · no console error · every word over its contrast floor | P27701–P27892 |
| C | panel geometry, measured on the running panels | P27893–P27916 |
| D | contrast, per control, both skins, before and after | P27917–P27960 |
| E | cross-panel traces — the same look decision in every place that shares it | P27961–P27990 |
| F | screenshots **actually looked at** (10), and an honest `⏭` for the 14 surfaces that were measured but not eyeballed | P27991–P28010 |
| G | judgment, and the guards | P28011–P28100 |

## Three faults this run found in its OWN probe before believing them

Recorded because each would otherwise have become a filed finding, and the ledger's own standing
lesson is that a guard which invents a failure protects nothing:

1. **`color(srgb …)` was unparsed.** Chrome serves `color-mix()` results in that spelling; the
   probe's rgba-only regex returned null and fell back to WHITE, reporting **white-on-white at
   1.07:1** on perfectly correct admin buttons.
2. **A `transparent` gradient stop was averaged in.** A sticky heading painted
   `linear-gradient(panel 70%, transparent)` came back at 1.07:1. Reading only the **opaque** stops
   gave its true 3.71. This is the trap `verify:look-ink` already documents, rediscovered.
3. **Playwright viewports never applied.** `newContext({width, height})` is silently ignored — it
   wants `viewport: {…}` — so a whole "phone" pass measured a 1280×720 desktop. Caught only because
   a probe printed `innerWidth` and it said 1280 for the A35 row.

An emoji's `color` was also being measured, which reported the 🍽️ in a top bar at 1.22:1. The font
paints those glyphs; the property says nothing about them.

## The run's own numbers

**500 new rows written and executed** — 401 ✅ · 24 ❌→✅ (found and fixed in this branch) ·
65 ⚠️ (measured, reported, deliberately not fixed — they are Part 4 decisions) · 10 ⏭.

**Seven problems found, seven fixed, one commit each.** Six of the seven were on the skin the
surface **opens in** — the panels' light and the guest's dark.

**The shape they nearly all share, and it is the same shape sweep #6 named:** a colour tuned to be a
FILL being used as INK, or a token that exists in one skin block and not the other. The new part is
*where* they were: **the places the previous run's guard had no probe for.** `verify:look-ink`
mounted `.brand` with no text of its own, so it measured the restaurant's name inside the panel
title and never the panel's own word; it had **no guest probe at all**, and three of this run's
faults were guest-side; and it never looked at the Take-order screen, which was carrying four.

Every one of the seven now has a probe or a static assertion. The guard also grew its first
styled-jsx check — **18 blocks that no guard in the repo had ever read** — and that one was proven
to bite before it was trusted.

# SWEEP #7 — T26, ROUND 2 (2026-08-31): the six the owner picked, and the re-test around them

He read the round-1 report and answered: *"idk how you will do 9th but do it also for tablet one do
10 th also can do 11,12,13,14 after donr with this plan whole 500 phases test around and fix if any
error found"* — so: build items 9–14, then plan and run a fresh 500-phase test around the changes
and fix whatever it turns up.

New ids **P46301–P46800**. (The block this round was going to use, `P28101`, was taken by T27 while
this branch was running — always re-check the registry at the moment of writing, not at the moment
of planning.)

## A correction that belongs at the top

**Round 1 told him the manager's phone floor showed "2 tables of 30". The steady figure was 4.** That
measurement was taken while the one-time printer prompt was on screen AND the connection pill was in
a degraded state. The fault was real and is fixed, but the number reported was worse than the truth,
and the ledger says so rather than quietly carrying the better number now.

## What the six turned out to be

| item | what it really was |
|---|---|
| **9** | NOT the floor header — the **top bar**. Six controls do not fit beside the brand at 360px, so it ran to two rows and tipped into a THIRD whenever the connection pill grew. The four set-once ones moved into the ☰ drawer (the waiter tablet's own pattern). Bar 152 → 103px, first tile 459 → 290px, **2 → 6 tables**. |
| **9 (tablet)** | **Nothing to do.** Its floor already starts 194px down and shows 6 — his own recorded 200px rule, already honoured. Reported as already-correct rather than changed. |
| **10** | The tablet's order screen went **14 → 1** controls under 44px and the manager's Take order **13 → 4**; every remainder is a control he has already ruled on (R4, R22, R40). |
| **11** | One token, 77 rules: 4.47 → 4.82 on the dark skin. |
| **12** | The joined-tile seat count: 4.44 → 6.71. |
| **13** | Two unreachable lines removed; the animation still runs. |
| **14** | 15 screens captured **and looked at** — and that is what found item 15. |

## Two things item 9 ran into, and neither was worked around quietly

- **R11** settled the floor header's KOT ▾ / QO/P layout (*"P four is perfectly fine"*) and **R25**
  settled the legend, three times. They are the rest of the gap to the tablet's 194px. The header
  was left exactly as it is and both were named in the report instead.
- **The CSS half alone would have hidden four buttons with nowhere to reach them**, so item 9 also
  touches `public/panels/editor/app.js` — the manager panel's file, not this territory's. Declared
  in the commit rather than hidden; no manager-panel terminal was running.

## What the re-test found on its own — items 15 to 20

**Six more faults, none of which any guard would have caught**, because `verify:look-ink` had no
probe on those screens:

- **16 — the Bills screen, where money is read.** NINE things under the line, including **the bill's
  own total at 2.03:1**. The July pass that fixed this family had deepened `.ord-pill.new` while the
  list actually renders `.ord-pill.received` (1.43:1 — an amber word on a tan pill), and left the
  states it did touch short of the line.
- **15** — "Read more" on a dish page, 4.29, from a token that was a plain alias of the border colour.
- **18** — the Editor's chosen category chip (2.71) and dish numbers (2.37).
- **19** — the Dashboard's up/down chips (3.53 / 3.86), and a seat count that had **drifted back**
  since sweep #6 set it in August.
- **20** — two table numbers losing to their own tile's tint.

Every one is now probed. **17** is that guard work.

## Three faults found in my OWN work before they shipped

1. The drawer builder **bailed out when its box already existed**, so a button injected after first
   paint never got a row — 3 of 4. Made re-entrant.
2. Item 18's first cut put the deeper mix on the BASE rule, which darkens **both** skins and took the
   dark chip from 7.57 to **2.46**. Caught by measuring both skins before committing.
3. Item 20's first value cleared the table NUMBER and left the table NAME short — one class, two
   floors, and measuring only one of them is exactly how the fault got there.

## The merge

Main moved **378 commits** while this branch ran. Three files conflicted and **both sides were kept
in every one**: the waiter-tablet terminal had raised tile FONT SIZES in the same rule where this
branch changed a COLOUR, and the two cache stamps were regenerated from the merged content rather
than one side's value being picked.

## The round's numbers

**500 rows written and executed** — 388 ✅ · 35 ❌→✅ · 62 ⚠️ · 15 ⏭.
**Twelve problems fixed across the two rounds' items 9–20, one commit each.**

Final live pass on the merged tree: **192 records, 0 sideways scroll, 0 leaked code text,
0 console errors, 0 driver errors.**

| P46322 | item 10: the manager's Take-order dish ✎ is 44px tall, width unchanged | measure .to-dish-edit | ❌→✅ | 40x40 → 40x44; the dish row keeps 21,172 of 21,332 px2 |

# SWEEP #7 — T26, ROUND 3 (2026-09-01): items 23 and 24, and a re-test of the WHOLE territory

He read the round-2 report and answered: *"can do the two things I give you the permission and also
plan again whole 500 physics test. Make sure you cover every single bit of things and within the
boundaries that you have been given the test and fix everything if you found anything."*

New ids **P46801–P47300**. **2000 rows now stand in this ledger.**

## The two he authorised

**Item 24 — the ☰ and the 🔔 on the manager bar.** Both were under the finger target (40 and 38) and
were the last two on that bar still short. The waiter tablet and the kitchen board settled this
control at 44px on 2026-08-22 and recorded why; the manager panel simply never followed. Honest
cost, and it is in the row: the bar grows 103 → 113px.

**Item 23 — the colour key moves into the ☰ menu on a phone.** Floor header **169 → 114px**, first
table **300 → 245px**, tables visible **4 → 6** — level with the waiter tablet at last. Above 760px
nothing moves at all.

### Neither rejection had to be reversed, and that is the finding

Item 23 was offered to him as *"a yes on reopening R11 or R25"*. Doing the work turned out to need
**neither**:

- **R11** forbids re-flowing the 🧾 KOT ▾ / ⚡ QO/P pair onto the title's line. That line is untouched
  at every width — a diff over `.floor-head` and `.floor-head-acts` comes back empty.
- **R25** keeps the legend SHORT: it forbids **adding** colours. Nothing was added. `const LEG` is
  untouched and the strip still reads Free · Preparing · Served · called · unpaid · paid, in that
  order. **It moved; it did not change.**

So both rows stay exactly as they are in `docs/REJECTED-IDEAS.md`, and **nothing was written to
`## Reversed`, because nothing was reversed.** A rejection you can honour and still get the win is
worth more than a rejection you talk him out of.

## What the whole-territory re-test found

The instruction was *cover every single bit*, so this round's surface list grew from 24 to **32**:
the three **☰ drawers** (which no sweep had ever opened), and six more console screens. Two of the
new ones came back with faults:

- **Item 25 — manager Platform.** Three, including **UNPAID**, the one word on a row that says money
  is still owed: 3.76 light **and** 3.93 dark. Plus an order line's ×N and the Print bill label, both
  gold-as-ink at 2.81.
- **Item 26 — admin Access.** The chosen tab put white on the console's bare blue-500 at **3.68:1** —
  the *same number on the same blue* this project already fixed on the sign-in pages, with the LIGHT
  console block already carrying blue-600 and only dark left behind.

**Both screens were clean in every previous sweep because no previous sweep had ever opened them.**

## The shape that keeps coming back

Six items this sweep are one fault wearing different clothes: **a colour tuned for one skin or one
surface, reused on another** — items 4, 7, 12, 21, 25 and 26. Four of them were half-finished
earlier fixes where one skin was corrected and the other was left.

## One fault of my own, caught before commit

The colour key was moved into the drawer correctly and then **never shown** — the phone rule set the
spacing but not `display`. Found by opening the drawer and MEASURING the strip's box instead of
trusting that it had moved.

## The round's numbers

**500 rows written and executed** — 418 ✅ · 11 ❌→✅ · 71 ⚠️ · 0 ⏭.

Final live pass, **32 surfaces × 4 viewports × 2 skins = 256 records**:
**0 contrast failures · 0 sideways scroll · 0 leaked code text · 0 console errors · 0 off-viewport ·
0 driver errors.** The static half is **42 checks, all passing, in under two seconds**.
